# Selenides

Units: T is in K; $Cp^{\circ}$ and $S^{\circ}$ are in cal/mol·K; $H^{\circ} - H_{298}^{\circ}$ , $\Delta Hf^{\circ}$ and $\Delta Gf^{\circ}$ are in kcal/mol.

All formation reactions for selenides use $\mathrm{Se}_2(\mathrm{g})$ . A formation table for $2\mathrm{Se}(\mathrm{c},\mathrm{l}) = \mathrm{Se}_2(\mathrm{g})$ is included.

Most data were from reference 23, except as follows:

$\mathrm{NiSe}_{1.05}(\mathrm{c})$ (22), $\mathrm{NiSe}_{1.14}(\mathrm{c})$ (22), $\mathrm{NiSe}_{1.25}(\mathrm{c})$ (22), $\mathrm{NiSe}_{2}(\mathrm{c})$ (22), and $\mathrm{PbSe}(\mathrm{c},1)$ (18, 30).

# Silicates

Units: I is in K; $Cp^{\circ}$ and $S^{\circ}$ are in cal/mol·K; $H^{\circ} - H_{298}^{\circ}$ , $\Delta Hf^{\circ}$ and $\Delta Gf^{\circ}$ are in kcal/mol.

Sources of data from general references are listed below, with the reference(s) given immediately after each formula:

$BaSiO_{3}(c)$ (26), $Ba_{2}SiO_{4}(c)$ (26), $Be_{2}SiO_{4}(c)$ (7), $Fe_{2}SiO_{4}(c,1)$ (31), $K_{2}SiO_{3}(c)$ (4), $MgSiO_{3}(c,1)$ (7, 26), $Mg_{2}SiO_{4}(c)$ (7, 26), $Na_{2}SiO_{3}(c,1)$ (7, 33), $Na_{2}Si_{2}O_{4}(c,1)$ (7, 33), $Ni_{2}SiO_{4}(c)$ (22), $ZrSiO_{4}(c)$ (7, 32).

Additional sources:

$\mathrm{Al}_{2}\mathrm{SiO}_{5}$ (kyanite), $\mathrm{Al}_{2}\mathrm{SiO}_{5}$ (andalusite), and $\mathrm{Al}_{2}\mathrm{SiO}_{5}$ (sillimanite):

Haas, J. L., Jr., G. R. Robinson, Jr., and B. S. Hemmingway. Thermodynamic Tabulations for Selected Phases in the System CaO-Al $_2$ O $_3$ -SiO $_2$ -H $_2$ O at 101.325 kPa (1 atm) Between 273.15 and 1800 K. J. Phys. and Chem. Ref. Data, v. 10, 1981, pp. 575-669.

$\mathrm{BaSiO_3(c)}$ and $\mathrm{Ba_2SiO_4(c)}$

Fegley, B., Jr. (MA Inst. Tech., Cambridge, MA). Private communication, 1981; available upon request from L. B. Pankratz, BuMines, Albany, OR.

Weller, W. W., and K. K. Kelley. Low-Temperature Heat Capacities and Entropies at 298.16° K of Crystalline Silicates of Barium and Strontium. BuMines RI 6556, 1964, 8 pp.

$\mathrm{CaSiO_3}$ (wollastonite):

Southard, J. C. A Modified Calorimeter for High Temperatures. The Heat Content of Silica, Wollastonite, and Thorium Dioxide Above 25°. J. Am. Chem. Soc., v. 63, 1941, pp. 3142-3146.

Torgeson, D. R., and Th. G. Sahama. A Hydrofluoric Acid Solution Calorimeter and the Determination of the Heats of Formation of $Mg_{2}SiO_{4}$ , $MgSiO_{3}$ , and $CaSiO_{3}$ . J. Am. Chem. Soc., v. 70, 1948, pp. 2156-2160.

Wagner, H. Zur Thermochemie der Metasilikate des Calciums und Magnesiums und des Diopsids (Thermochemistry of the Metasilicates of Calcium and Magnesium and of Diopside). Z. Anorg. und Allg. Chem., v. 208, 1932, pp. 1-12.

CaSiO $_{3}$ (pseudowollastonite):

Haas, J. L., Jr., G. R. Robinson, Jr., and B. S. Hemmingway. Thermodynamic Tabulations for Selected Phases in the System CaO-Al₂O₃-SiO₂-H₂O at 101.325 kPa (1 atm) Between 273.15 and 1800 K. J. Phys. and Chem. Ref. Data, v. 10, 1981, pp. 575-669.

$\mathrm{Fe}_{2}\mathrm{SiO}_{4}(\mathrm{c},1)$ :

Orr, R. L. High-Temperature Heat Contents of Magnesium Orthosilicate and Ferrous Orthosilicate. J. Am. Chem. Soc., v. 75, 1953, pp. 528-529.

Robie, R. A., C. B. Finch, and B. S. Hemmingway. Heat Capacity and Entropy of Fayalite ( $Fe_{2}SiO_{4}$ ) Between 5.1 and 383 K: Comparison of Calorimetric and Equilibrium Values for the QFM Buffer Reaction. Am. Mineral., v. 67, 1982, pp. 463-469.

$K_{2}SiO_{3}(c)$ and $K_{2}Si_{2}O_{5}(c,1)$ :

Beyer, R. P., M. J. Ferrante, and G. E. Daut. Thermodynamic Properties of Potassium Metasilicate and Disilicate. BuMines RI 8410, 1980, 21 pp.

$\mathrm{Li}_{2}\mathrm{SiO}_{3}(\mathrm{c})$ and $\mathrm{Li}_{2}\mathrm{Si}_{2}\mathrm{O}_{5}(\mathrm{c})$

Bennington, K. O., M. J. Ferrante, and J. M. Stuve. Thermodynamic Properties of Two Lithium Silicates, $Li_{2}SiO_{3}$ and $Li_{2}Si_{2}O_{5}$ . BuMines RI 8187, 1976, 19 pp.

$MnSiO_{3}(c)$ :

Kelley, K. K. The Specific Heats at Low Temperatures of Ferrous Silicate, Manganous Silicate, and Zirconium Silicate. J. Am. Chem. Soc., v. 63, 1941, pp. 2750-2752.

King, E. G. Heats of Formation of Manganous Metasilicate (Rhodonite) and Ferrous Orthosilicate (Fayalite). J. Am. Chem. Soc., v. 74, 1952, pp. 4446-4448.

Southard, J. G., and G. E. Moore. High-Temperature Heat Contents of Manganese Teroxide, Manganese Silicate, and Manganese Carbide. J. Am. Chem. Soc., v. 64, 1942, pp. 1769-1770.

# Silicides

Units: T is in K; $Cp^{0}$ and $S^{0}$ are in cal/mol·K; $H^{0} - H_{298}^{0}$ , $\Delta Hf^{0}$ and $\Delta Gf^{0}$ are in kcal/mol.   
Sources of data from general references are listed below, with the reference given immediately after each formula:   
CoSi(c,l) (31), CoSi₂(c,l) (31), FeSi(c,l) (16, 31), FeSi₂(c) (16, 31), Fe₃Si(c) (16, 31), MnSi(c,l) (1), MnSi₁.₇(c) (1), Mn₃Si(c) (1), Mn₅Si₃(c,l) (1), MoSi₂(c) (2), Mo₃Si(c) (2), Mo₅Si₃(c) (2), NiSi(c,l) (22), Ni₁.₀₄Si₁.₉₃(c) (22), Ni₂Si(c,l) (22), TaSi₂(c) (1).
